> AGENT

Yes. The current daily update flow is in [summariser_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/summariser_cog.py:28) and [summariser.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/summariser.py:2218).

High-level read: moving from daily to hourly should not be “run the daily summary every hour.” That would spam, repeat itself, and fight the current date-based storage model. It should become more like an **hourly editorial decision loop**.

The shape I’d recommend:

1. **Run hourly, but evaluate a rolling window**
   Use something like the last 6 hours of activity as the candidate pool, not just the last 1 hour. That lets the bot wait for reactions, replies, and follow-up context before deciding something is worth posting.

2. **Pass recent published updates into the prompt**
   Before generating, fetch the last 6-12 posted updates from storage and include them as “already covered.” The model should be told:
   - do not repeat these topics
   - only revisit something if there is meaningful new information
   - if revisiting, frame it as a continuation, not a fresh announcement
   - prefer silence over weak repetition

3. **Add an explicit “should post?” gate**
   The first LLM call should probably not be “write the update.” It should classify candidates:
   - `post_now`
   - `wait_for_more_signal`
   - `skip_duplicate`
   - `skip_low_signal`
   - `merge_with_existing_thread/topic`

   Only `post_now` becomes a Discord post.

4. **Use community validation signals**
   The DB already has useful fields like `reaction_count`, `reactors`, `reference_id`, and reply/thread context utilities in [weekly_digest.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/scripts/weekly_digest.py:294). For hourly updates, I’d weight:
   - unique reactors, not just raw reactions
   - replies/comments after the original post
   - continued discussion over several messages
   - media/examples that appeared after the initial announcement
   - whether multiple people are engaging, not just the author posting a sequence

5. **Change storage from daily rows to update events**
   Right now summaries are stored in `daily_summaries` with date/channel uniqueness in [storage_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/storage_handler.py:393). Hourly needs a new table or mode, something like `community_updates`, with:
   - `posted_at`
   - `lookback_start`, `lookback_end`
   - `topic_key` or semantic fingerprint
   - source message IDs
   - posted Discord message IDs
   - decision status: posted/skipped/waiting
   - reason

6. **Be comfortable posting nothing**
   This is the key product change. The hourly job should frequently no-op. I’d avoid fallback posts like “No significant activity” for hourly mode. Silence is better.

7. **Keep daily summary separately, at least initially**
   I would not replace daily updates immediately. I’d add hourly “notable updates” as a separate mode, then let the daily summary become a recap of what mattered plus anything the hourly loop missed.

The main code changes would be: replace the fixed daily scheduler with an hourly loop, parameterize the lookback window, add recent-update retrieval, add a candidate/decision phase before generation, and stop using date-only uniqueness for this path.

Conceptually: hourly updates should behave less like a digest and more like a patient community editor that asks, “Has this become interesting enough yet, and have we already told people?”

> AGENT

Yes, I think you want to store **editorial memory**, not just posted summaries.

Right now storage is basically: “for this date/channel, here is the summary we posted.” For hourly, you need storage that can remember “we noticed this, decided not to post yet, and should check back later.”

**Storage Changes**

I’d add a new table, separate from `daily_summaries`, maybe `community_update_candidates` or `editorial_update_items`.

Each row represents a possible story/topic, whether or not it was posted.

Fields I’d want:

```text
id
guild_id
topic_key
status
first_seen_at
last_seen_at
last_evaluated_at
next_check_at
source_message_ids
source_channel_ids
posted_message_ids
related_update_ids
title
summary
editorial_notes
decision_reason
community_signal_snapshot
created_at
updated_at
```

Where `status` could be:

```text
watching
ready_to_post
posted
skipped_low_signal
skipped_duplicate
skipped_stale
superseded
```

The most important new field is probably `editorial_notes`.

That lets the bot persist thoughts like:

```text
This looks promising because Kijai shared a new workflow, but there are no replies yet. Recheck in 2-3 hours for reactions, follow-up examples, or questions from others.
```

Or:

```text
This was already covered in the 14:00 update. Only mention again if there is a concrete release link, benchmark, fix, or strong community discussion.
```

I’d also add a second table for actual posted updates, maybe `community_updates`, unless you want one table with posted candidates. It would store:

```text
id
guild_id
posted_at
lookback_start
lookback_end
discord_channel_id
discord_message_ids
full_update_json
short_text
source_message_ids
topic_keys
dev_mode
```

This gives the hourly job two kinds of memory:

1. **Published memory**: what has already been said.
2. **Editorial memory**: what is being watched, skipped, or waiting for validation.

That second one is the big difference.

**Prompt Changes**

The current prompt is mostly: “Here are messages from the last 24 hours, extract newsworthy items.” It does not really have a durable editorial state. It can avoid duplicates inside one chunk, but not across runs.

For hourly, I’d split the prompt flow into two phases.

**Phase 1: Editorial Triage**

Input:

```text
Recent activity from last 6 hours
Previously posted updates from last 6-12 hours
Open watched candidates from previous runs
Current reaction/reply snapshots
```

Output structured JSON like:

```json
[
  {
    "[REDACTED],
    "decision": "watch",
    "confidence": 0.62,
    "reason": "Interesting workflow share, but only the author has posted about it so far and there are no replies yet.",
    "what_to_watch_for": [
      "replies from other users trying it",
      "additional examples",
      "clarification that this is reusable/released"
    ],
    "next_check_hours": 2,
    "source_message_ids": ["..."]
  }
]
```

Possible decisions:

```text
post_now
watch
skip_duplicate
skip_low_signal
skip_stale
merge_with_existing
```

This phase should be explicitly allowed to post nothing.

**Phase 2: Write Update**

Only for `post_now` items.

Input:

```text
Approved topics
Relevant source messages
Related previous updates
Editorial notes
```

The writing prompt should say:

```text
If this topic has been mentioned before, write it as a continuation.
Do not present it as brand new unless the new evidence justifies that.
Include why it matters now: replies, reactions, release, follow-up examples, or concrete validation.
```

**Yes, Pass Notes Forward**

Absolutely. The bot should pass forward notes like:

```text
Seems interesting, but wait for community reaction.
```

That is exactly the missing thing.

The notes should not just be invisible chain-of-thought. They should be explicit stored editorial state, because the next hourly run needs them. Think of them as an assignment desk:

```text
Topic: New ComfyUI node from X
Current state: promising but unvalidated
Watch for: other people testing it, reactions above 5 unique users, author posting repo link
Do not post unless: there is a concrete example or meaningful discussion
```

Then on the next run, the model sees:

```text
Previously you were watching this. Here is what changed since then.
```

That lets it decide:

```text
Still watching
Now worth posting
No longer interesting
Duplicate of something already posted
```

**Prompt Policy I’d Add**

The hourly prompt needs stronger editorial rules than the daily one:

```text
You are not required to post an update.
Most hours should produce no public update.
Prefer waiting when the only evidence is one person posting something.
Post when there is a meaningful change, community validation, a useful release, or a clear follow-up.
Do not summarize general chat.
Do not repeat previously posted topics unless there is new information.
If repeating, explicitly frame it as an update to the earlier item.
```

**The Big Design Shift**

Daily summaries ask:

```text
What happened in the last 24 hours?
```

Hourly updates should ask:

```text
Has anything matured enough to tell the community now?
```

So yes: store the previous editorial judgments, the “watch this later” notes, and the conditions that would make something post-worthy. Without that, hourly summaries will either spam or become repetitive.

> DEVELOPER

And could this do, you think we should have like a new table or could we somehow adapt the current daily updates table we have to do this. I think we should have it in the, this is instead of the daily summary so we should not keep both of them.

> AGENT

If this is replacing the daily summary, I still would **not** try to stretch `daily_summaries` as-is. I’d either rename/generalize it or add a new replacement table and migrate over. The current table is built around the wrong unit: `date + channel_id`. Hourly editorial updates need the unit to be **a topic/candidate/update event over time**.

My recommendation: create a new generalized table and retire `daily_summaries`.

Something like:

```text
community_updates
```

It would store both things the daily table currently stores and the new hourly editorial state.

Suggested shape:

```text
id
guild_id
channel_id
update_type              -- hourly, manual, legacy_daily, maybe recap
status                   -- watching, skipped, posted, superseded
topic_key
title
full_summary
short_summary
editorial_notes
decision_reason
what_to_watch_for
source_message_ids
source_channel_ids
posted_discord_message_ids
related_update_ids
lookback_start
lookback_end
first_seen_at
last_seen_at
last_evaluated_at
next_check_at
posted_at
included_in_main_summary -- probably removable if no daily main summary
dev_mode
created_at
updated_at
```

The key change is that rows are no longer “the summary for May 8.” They are “this topic/update candidate exists, here’s what we currently think about it.”

I would avoid adapting `daily_summaries` directly because:

- `date` uniqueness fights hourly posting.
- `channel_id` is not enough, because a topic can span channels.
- skipped/watched candidates need to be stored even when nothing is posted.
- previous editorial notes need durable state.
- a single hourly run may post zero, one, or several updates.
- “already covered” should be topic-based, not calendar-day-based.

You could technically add columns to `daily_summaries`, but it would become misleading fast. You’d end up with a table called daily summaries that contains hourly watched-but-not-posted editorial candidates. That confusion will leak into code.

I’d do this transition:

1. Add `community_updates`.
2. Change the summariser to write/read from `community_updates`.
3. Stop writing new rows to `daily_summaries`.
4. Optionally migrate old daily summaries into `community_updates` with `update_type = 'legacy_daily'`.
5. Keep `daily_summaries` read-only for a while, then remove it once nothing depends on it.

So yes, if hourly replaces daily, make the replacement table. Don’t preserve both product flows, but do preserve old data either by migration or historical read-only access.

> DEVELOPER

Any other decisions we need to make before going ahead with this?

> AGENT

Yes. Before implementing, I’d make these decisions explicitly:

**1. What counts as “worth posting”**
Define the threshold. For example:

```text
Post if there is either:
- a concrete release/link/workflow/example people can use
- meaningful follow-up discussion
- reactions from at least N unique people
- multiple members independently engaging with it
- a clear update to a previously watched topic
```

Without this, the model will drift between too quiet and too noisy.

**2. How quiet hourly mode should be**
Decide expected frequency. My instinct: most hours should post nothing. Maybe target something like **2-6 updates/day**, not 24.

**3. Lookback window**
I’d start with:

```text
Activity window: last 6 hours
Previous posted updates shown to prompt: last 12-24 hours
Watched candidates shown to prompt: active items from last 24-48 hours
```

**4. Candidate lifecycle**
Decide when watched items expire.

Example:

```text
watching -> posted
watching -> skipped_stale after 24h with no signal
watching -> superseded if a better related candidate appears
posted -> can be updated only if meaningful new evidence appears
```

**5. Whether updates are per-channel or server-wide**
Daily currently does per-channel summaries and combines them. Hourly should probably be **server-wide topic selection**, because hourly per-channel summaries would be noisy.

**6. Posting format**
Do hourly updates go into the same summary channel as standalone posts? A single rolling thread? A daily thread?  
I’d recommend standalone posts in the summary channel, grouped only when multiple related items are ready at the same time.

**7. Whether to keep top generations/top art sharing**
The current daily run also posts top generations, top art sharing, speaker welcomes, social pick DMs. If daily is removed, decide whether those become:
- separate scheduled jobs
- part of hourly editorial selection
- removed

I would not fold all of them into hourly summarising automatically.

**8. How deterministic the model output should be**
I’d make the editorial phase return strict JSON decisions, not prose. Then code enforces “no post” unless decision is `post_now`.

**9. Migration plan**
Do we migrate old `daily_summaries` into `community_updates` as `legacy_daily`, or just leave the old table read-only? I’d migrate only if we want old summaries to inform duplicate detection.

**10. Admin visibility**
Useful to decide whether skipped/watched decisions are visible somewhere. Even a simple admin command like “show watched update candidates” would help debug why it did or didn’t post.

My default choices would be: server-wide hourly editorial loop, 6-hour activity window, 24-hour duplicate memory, watched candidates expire after 24 hours, strict JSON triage first, post nothing by default, and separate jobs for top generations/top art/speaker welcomes.

> AGENT

Yes, I think the better architecture is an **agentic live-update editor**, not an hourly summarizer.

The job should not be “every hour, summarize recent messages.” It should be “on a schedule, inspect what may be worth surfacing, investigate enough context, then either publish a live update or record why it chose not to.”

**Core Product Shape**
One public channel, renamed from daily summaries to something like `live-updates`.

The bot posts directly in that channel, no per-channel summary threads, no daily thread. It behaves like a curated community wire:

```text
New release / workflow / discovery
Notable community creation
Follow-up to something previously watched
Highly-reacted generation / artwork
```

Most runs may produce no public post. That is fine. The output is either:

```text
posted update
```

or:

```text
no public post, but internal audit/candidate state updated
```

**Two Related Systems**
I’d split this into two live systems:

1. **Live Updates**
   Curated editorial posts in the renamed summaries channel. These are written updates with context.

2. **Top Creations**
   More automatic surfacing of highly-reacted visual/art posts into the top generations/top creations channel. This can be less editorial and more threshold-driven, though still deduped.

So a cool generation might go to `top-creations` once it hits the threshold, while a deeper story about a workflow/tool/release goes to `live-updates`.

**Agentic Workflow**
Instead of one LLM prompt over all messages, I’d give the agent tools/context and let it investigate.

A run could look like:

1. Fetch recent candidate messages from last N hours.
2. Fetch open watchlist items.
3. Fetch recent posted updates.
4. Score/rank candidate topics.
5. For promising ones, investigate:
   - message thread/replies
   - surrounding messages
   - author profile/reputation
   - previous posts about same topic
   - whether this was already featured
   - reactions over time
   - related media/examples
6. Decide:
   - post now
   - watch longer
   - skip
   - mark duplicate
   - update previous topic
7. Either publish markdown or store editorial notes.

This means the prompt becomes less “write a summary” and more “you are an editor with tools; investigate before publishing.”

**Author Credibility**
Yes, reputation should be part of the context, but it should not become a hard popularity contest.

Good proxies:

```text
total messages
total reactions received
average reactions per post
median reactions per post
top percentile among members
number of posts with 5+ reactions
number of posts previously featured
ratio of reacted posts to total posts
recent reaction trend
whether other high-reputation members reply/react
whether they have shipped/shared useful things before
```

I’d especially use **median reactions per post** and **percentile rank**, because averages can be distorted by one viral post.

But credibility should be used as a weighting signal, not a gate. A low-history person can still post something important. A high-reputation person can still post something not worth amplifying.

The agent should see something like:

```text
Author context:
- 312 total posts
- median reactions/post: 3
- average reactions/post: 7.8
- top 4% by average reactions
- 18 previous posts above 10 reactions
- 3 previous live updates featured this author
```

Then it can reason: “this person’s early post deserves watching even before the community fully reacts.”

**Rubric**
I agree: reuse the existing rubric, but adapt it from daily digest to live editorial judgment.

The existing prompt already prioritizes:
- original community contributions
- notable demos
- high engagement
- new features/tools/announcements
- shared workflows
- AI art/open source projects
- constructive framing

For live mode, I’d add:

```text
Post only when there is a reason people benefit from seeing this now.
Prefer concrete usefulness over general activity.
A single promising post can become a watch item.
Community reaction can validate, clarify, or disconfirm.
If the item was already covered, only post if there is meaningful new information.
```

**Watchlist**
The watchlist should be first-class.

The agent should be able to create/update a candidate like:

```json
{
  "status": "watching",
  "[REDACTED],
  "title": "Pajai shared a promising new control workflow",
  "editorial_notes": "Looks technically interesting and author has strong prior signal, but no one has tried it yet. Watch for examples, repo/workflow link, or replies from users testing it.",
  "what_to_watch_for": [
    "additional examples",
    "workflow link",
    "questions or successful tests from other members"
  ],
  "next_check_at": "...",
  "expires_at": "..."
}
```

And yes, I’d let the agent choose the watch duration, within bounds. For example:

```text
minimum: 1 hour
default: 6-12 hours
maximum: 48 hours unless explicitly renewed
```

On each run, the agent sees what is expiring and decides whether to post, renew, or close it.

**Storage**
I’d make one replacement storage model, not keep the daily one as active.

Tables I’d want:

```text
community_updates
```

For public posts and final decisions.

```text
community_update_candidates
```

For watchlist/editorial state.

```text
community_update_runs
```

For auditability of each scheduled agent run.

Possibly:

```text
member_update_stats
```

Either materialized/cached, or computed periodically.

Candidate rows store the living editorial state. Update rows store what was actually published. Run rows store what the agent looked at and why it decided what it decided.

That gives admin visibility and lets us debug “why didn’t it post this?”

**Prompt Design**
I’d use several prompts or structured steps:

1. **Candidate discovery**
   “Find things potentially worth investigating.”

2. **Investigation planner**
   “For each promising item, what context do you need?”

3. **Editorial decision**
   “Given source activity, prior updates, watchlist notes, and author/context metrics, decide post/watch/skip.”

4. **Writer**
   “Write the public update in markdown.”

The final writer should only receive approved topics, not the entire raw firehose.

**Update Types**
I’d explicitly support multiple update types:

```text
news_item
tool_or_workflow
community_creation
follow_up
release_or_link
discussion_highlight
top_creation
```

They should write differently. A release needs context and usefulness. A community creation can be more like “go look at this.” A follow-up should reference what changed since the earlier item.

**Posting**
For live updates:

- post only in `live-updates`
- no per-channel posts
- no threads
- markdown directly in channel
- include source links
- include media when useful

For top creations:

- post in `top-creations`
- triggered by reaction threshold and dedupe
- likely no long editorial text needed
- can be ongoing/live

**Big Refactor Summary**
The current system is a scheduled summarizer. The new system should be a persistent editorial agent.

The main shift:

```text
old:
messages -> summary -> post -> store daily row

new:
messages -> candidates -> investigation -> editorial decision -> watch/skip/post -> audit trail
```

That is a much better fit for what you’re describing. It gives the bot memory, patience, context, author reputation, and the ability to say “not yet.”

Implemented pieces include:

- New live editor core: [live_update_editor.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/live_update_editor.py)
- Live top creations flow: [live_top_creations.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/live_top_creations.py)
- Prompt helpers: [live_update_prompts.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/live_update_prompts.py)
- Runtime wiring replacing the daily loop: [summariser_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/summariser_cog.py)
- Storage/DB wrappers: [storage_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/storage_handler.py), [db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py)
- Supabase migration in sibling repo: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/supabase/migrations/20260508160000_live_update_editor.sql`
- New focused tests for lifecycle, publishing/splitting, storage, runtime wiring, admin/health, prompts, and top creations.

Validation:

- Focused live-update tests: `49 passed`
- Full suite: `283 passed, 19 skipped`
- Executor artifact also reports compile checks, `git diff --check`, and a throwaway split-feed repro passed.

Important caveat: the worktree also has `.megaplan` metadata/schema edits from the harness itself. The app implementation and migration are present and tests pass.

> AGENT

Mostly robust at the code/test level, but not fully production-proven yet.

What I verified:

- Full test suite passes: `283 passed, 19 skipped`.
- Focused live-update tests pass: lifecycle, no-op/skipped runs, duplicate suppression, split Discord messages with ordered `discord_message_ids`, storage wrappers, runtime wiring, admin/health, prompt helpers, top creations.
- The migration file exists at:
  `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/supabase/migrations/20260508160000_live_update_editor.sql`
- The code no longer defaults to the 07:00 daily loop; it wires live updates and live top creations instead.
- `daily_summaries` is now treated as legacy/history in admin/debug surfaces.

What I have **not** verified:

- I have **not run the migration against a real local/staging/prod database**.
- I checked `supabase status`, but the local Supabase DB container is not running:
  `No such container: supabase_db_bndc`
- I have **not done a live Discord smoke test** where the bot archives fresh messages, runs the live editor, posts to the live-updates channel, and records feed items in Supabase.

So: I’d call this a strong implementation pass with good automated coverage, but I would not call it deployed-safe until we do:

1. Start/reset local Supabase or apply to staging.
2. Run the new migration.
3. Run a one-shot live editor pass in dev mode.
4. Confirm rows appear in `live_update_editor_runs`, `live_update_candidates`, `live_update_decisions`, `live_update_feed_items`.
5. Confirm an overlong update stores ordered message IDs.
6. Confirm the bot posts to the configured summary/live-updates channel and top creations posts separately.

The main remaining risk is deployment/runtime integration, not unit-level behavior.

Hello there, You can read about what we're up to banodoco.ai, particualrly the roadmap page: htt
Request

Publish
Don't show again
Hello there, You can read about what we're up to banodoco.ai, particualrly the roadmap page: https://banodoco.ai/Roadmap_&_collaboration In short, we want to build towards 3 components in an open-source manner - and are looking for both FT & passive collaborators. In all areas, people are know a lot more than me are very appreciated 🙂 The components are: - Steerable Motion: the model to create key frames that you probably came across to find your way here. You can find more detail on it (here)[https://banodoco.ai/Steerable_Motion] and learn about the projects in ⁠new_channels. - Img+Img2Vid models: repurposing existing text2video models like ModelScope or AnimatedDIff to work based on 2 input images - the starting and ending ones. Additionally, we'll explore training our own model to do this - once we have money that is. You can join ⁠dev_updates for more information here. - Creative Assistant: this will be an LLM that takes user queries, parses them, and channels them to models. We won't be actioning this just yet unless someone is very enthusiastic about LLaMA2 - you can find more information on ⁠unknown. - Product:  - Research Product: this product is for us to test the models and approaches as we build towards the 'proper' product - @lone_samurai and I have been working on it for the past few months and should have a production version ready shortly. We're looking for someone who can help get this running on local GPUs - more detail in ⁠daily_summaries - Production Product: this is the full, slick product that will leverage all of the above - we're not actioning this just yet, but you can message me with some stuff you made before if you might be interested in helping with it. That's it! Thanks for reading - please feel free to message me if you have any ideas, ⁠introductions or talk in the ⁠welcome channel.
Banodoco
Banodoco - Empowering the Open Source AI Art Ecosystem
Building tools and nurturing a culture to inspire, empower and reward open collaboration in the AI and digital art ecosystem.

Hi there, The only project that's probably relevant to start right now is ⁠ad_fine-tuning - seeing as we don't have money for large training runs yet. If you're interested in potentially help figure that out, please check out that channel. You can also join ⁠unknown channel if you're interested in thinking/talking about how we might train our own model. You can also jump ⁠unknown for general chat on this topic.

Hi everyone, I am a VJ and have a very specific need for a way to create relatively short animat
Event

Hi everyone, I am a VJ and have a very specific need for a way to create relatively short animations that hit specific keyframes at specific rythmic key points, as well as return to the original starting frame at the end of the short loop, which I think a good early target use case for a project like this. I have experience with after effects, cinema4d, some traditional hand animation experience, thousands of hours of professional live VJing experience here in Vegas so I have a lot of very specific generative AI functionality needs that I haven't yet found a solution for. I also have some, though limited, software developer experience, web developer experience, some basic openCV experience. And over the past 6 months ive been experimenting with all sorts of LLM stuff and various other locally run AI stuff, but I havent yet attempted to train my own model or or train a LoRA. I would love to contribute skills or feedback that I could towards this project if there is a way that I could be of some use to make the goal you have a reality. I've already tasted the possibilities this kind of technology could have on VJing after I used over 100 Runway Gen 2 clips I generated the morning before an event, at an event, on a big movie theater screen. But not having an ability to control the action, the timing, or the loopability of it severely limits the usefulness of the clip and agency I have when mixing the visuals live. One day the technology will get to the point in which we will be able to whip up an entire concert experience in a few days before an event, with the flexibility to iterate at the same rate as a DJ updates their setlists, thats what gets me excited.
Share with your followers!
Publish

and qay faster, every 5 minutes instead of every hour

> AGENT

Yes: dev should run the editor every 5 minutes, independent of the hourly archive loop. I’m going to encode that as `dev_mode` behavior in `SummarizerCog`: dev loop interval 5 minutes, dry-run persistence, no top-creations persistence/checkpoint memory, and production loop disabled unless an env flag opts it in.

> AGENT

I’m editing this so the dangerous behavior cannot recur by default: production live loops require `LIVE_UPDATES_ENABLED=true`; dev mode runs every 5 minutes with `dry_run=True`, which publishes to the dev-configured channels but never writes live-update run/candidate/feed/checkpoint rows.

I’ve made the config split in code. The important defaults now are: production live loops are off unless the env explicitly enables them; dev mode runs both loops every 5 minutes; dev mode uses dry-run persistence so it can post previews but won’t write runs, candidates, feed items, duplicate state, or checkpoints.

> AGENT

Production is rolled back and healthy now.

- Active production deployment: `9ebf2d75-98cf-42c0-8dee-23197e4e7391`
- Status: Railway `SUCCESS`
- `/ready` and `/health`: OK
- Bad deployments: removed
- Recent rollback logs: no live-update/top-creation posting lines

What happened:
- The new live systems started with blank checkpoints.
- `LiveTopCreations` treated old archived `art_sharing` posts as unseen and reposted them.
- The live-update editor also used an invalid default model, `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022`, then fell back to heuristic generation.
- Runtime admin chat was already `claude-opus-4-6`; I patched live updates to use `claude-opus-4-6` too.

I patched the dev/prod split locally:

- Production live loops are disabled by default unless `LIVE_UPDATES_ENABLED=true` / `LIVE_TOP_CREATIONS_ENABLED=true`.
- Dev mode runs every 5 minutes.
- Dev mode is dry-run: it can post preview messages, but writes no live-update runs, candidates, feed items, duplicate state, or checkpoints.
- Dev mode does not take previous sends into account.
- Dev channel resolution now prefers `DEV_SUMMARY_CHANNEL_ID`, `DEV_TOP_GENS_CHANNEL_ID`, `DEV_ART_CHANNEL_ID`.
- Added `BOT_DEV_MODE=true` so Railway dev can run `python main.py` without changing the start command.

Testing:
- Focused live-update tests: `19 passed`
- `git diff --check`: clean
- Full suite had one unrelated Hypothesis slow-generation health-check fail, and that single test passed on rerun.

Current Railway only has a `production` environment. For dev testing, we should create/link a Railway `development` environment and set at least:

```text
BOT_DEV_MODE=true
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=<dev bot token>
DEV_SUMMARY_CHANNEL_ID=<dev live updates channel>
DEV_TOP_GENS_CHANNEL_ID=<dev top creations channel>
DEV_ART_CHANNEL_ID=<dev art sharing channel>
```

Then deploy the current branch to `development` only, not production.

args.lookback_hours,\n+        )\n+        await _run_pass(editor, top_creations, trigger=\"local_dev_once\" if args.once else \"local_dev_scheduled\")\n+        while not args.once:\n+            await asyncio.sleep(max(1.0, args.interval_minutes * 60.0))\n+            await _run_pass(editor, top_creations, trigger=\"local_dev_scheduled\")\n+    finally:\n+        await > AGENT

Killed the local runner. No `run_live_update_dev.py` process is running now.

I checked the old code from `origin/main` / GitHub-backed commit `1b09c830` and ported the important bits:

- Top generations now use the old bar: recent window only, video attachments only, non-NSFW channels, exclude art channel, `5+` unique reactions.
- Top art is handled as art first, before video classification, so art-channel media does not get mislabeled as generic top generation.
- Post format now matches the old compact style:
  - `By **author** in #channel`
  - `🔥 N unique reactions`
  - optional short quote
  - bare media URL on its own line, so Discord embeds it correctly
  - `🔗 Original post: ...`
- Removed the spammy `## Top Art Share` / list-like formatting.
- Tightened the live-update LLM rubric to use the old daily/social-picks criteria and prefer returning zero candidates over low-quality filler.
- Live-update posts now include one bare media URL and an original-post link, without category labels like “Project Update” cluttering the output.

Verification:
- `python -m py_compile scripts/run_live_update_dev.py`
- `pytest tests/test_live_runtime_wiring.py tests/test_live_top_creations.py tests/test_live_update_editor_publishing.py tests/test_live_update_prompts.py`
- Result: `19 passed`
- `git diff --check`: clean

I did not restart the local dev loop.

The news prompt seems to be picking up completely different things than what we were picking up before. That's the problem. It seems to be picking up very minor things that aren't very significant. You know, that are just almost irrelevant. Ooh, thanks.

I’ll tighten the news/live-update candidate prompt against the old summary criteria, not just the top-creation formatting. The issue is likely that the current prompt says “questions, requests, events” too broadly, so it’s elevating minor chatter that the old daily summary would have filtered out.

> AGENT

I’m going to tighten the live-news selection now, because the failure mode is clear: it inherited the hourly machinery but not the old daily summary’s editorial bar. > AGENT

Yes, that diagnosis is right. The new live-news prompt was too permissive, especially around `question`, `request`, and generic `project_update`, so it was treating ordinary chatter as feed-worthy.

I tightened it in `src/features/summarising/live_update_prompts.py`:

- Restored the old daily-summary editorial bar: new releases, tools, workflows, custom nodes, models, LoRAs, scripts, notable milestones, impressive demos, or strongly validated creations.
- Explicitly rejects thanks, intros, vague “working on X”, minor experiments, ordinary help requests, Discord/admin boilerplate, and low-signal status updates.
- Limits the model to at most `1-3` candidates and tells it to return an empty candidate list when there is no significant news.
- Added reaction count, reply count, channel name, and author name into the prompt payload so it has the same kind of context the old system used.
- Added a local post-LLM editorial gate, so even if the model selects weak chatter, code rejects it before posting.
- Raised publish confidence threshold from `0.45` to `0.65`.

I also added a regression test for the exact failure mode: the model tries to select minor “working on frame analysis” chatter and a generic prompt-help request, and the generator now returns no candidates.

Verification passed:

```text
22 passed
python compile passed
git diff --check passed
```

> DEVELOPER

And does it have the tools to search earlier for context on both the earlier messags and poster? This feels wildly incoerent

> AGENT

No. It does not currently have tool-style search. That is a real gap, not a model issue.

The current live editor is a one-shot LLM call over a recent message packet plus limited author snapshot fields and saved memory/watchlist rows. It cannot decide “let me look up this poster’s prior work”, “has this topic already been covered?”, or “what happened earlier in this channel/thread?” unless that context was already stuffed into the prompt. That’s why it can feel incoherent: it is judging isolated recent snippets.

> AGENT

No. Current state:

- It is using `claude-opus-4-6` for the live editor.
- It is **not** using the exact old daily-news prompt.
- It is **not** agentic/tool-using.
- It does **not** search earlier messages or poster history on demand.
- It was fetching all guild messages, but only sending the first 50 into the prompt. I changed that to 200.
- It was not explicitly filtering NSFW in the live-news fetch. I added channel context + NSFW filtering.
- Dev runner is stopped.

The duplicate you pasted happened because I ran a one-shot, then relaunched the dry-run process immediately. Since dev intentionally does not persist/check previous sends, it posted the same source again. That part is explainable. The bad selection is the bigger issue.

The coherent version should be a two-stage process:

1. **Candidate scan** over the last hour or six hours across all non-NSFW channels.
2. **Context expansion** for promising candidates:
   - earlier messages in same thread/channel/topic
   - poster’s recent messages
   - poster reputation/average reactions
   - whether topic/source was already covered
   - replies/reactions after initial post
3. Then Opus decides whether to publish, defer/watch, or skip.

Right now it is mostly doing step 1 and asking Opus to infer too much from a flat packet. That is why it feels stupid. I would not relaunch it until we add the context-expansion pass.

The editorial subagent confirmed the same failure: keywords were being treated as newsworthiness, and the final publish gate was too weak. I’m applying the hard gates now: valid source IDs required, confidence floor up to `0.85`, one normal post per run, stronger project/showcase criteria, and title/body overlap rejection.

> AGENT

Implemented the editor-context version and relaunched dev.

What changed:

- Opus live editor is still `claude-opus-4-6`.
- Prompt now uses the old daily-summary editorial rules directly: novelty, community usefulness, evidence, attribution, no hype, media guidance, skip if weak.
- Agent output is now decision-first: `publish`, `defer`, `skip`, `duplicate`, with evidence/context fields.
- It now receives:
  - current source-window messages
  - recent feed items from the last 24h
  - editorial memory/watchlist, including in dev reads
  - same-channel history
  - author recent messages
  - author stats/reaction averages
  - guidance on how to use that context
- Production path still persists candidates/decisions/feed/checkpoints.
- Dev path still does not write live-editor DB state, but now reads the real context so it behaves closer to prod.
- Hard gates added:
  - no valid source IDs means reject
  - final publish confidence is now `0.85`
  - one normal live update max per run
  - generic ComfyUI/model/workflow keyword chatter no longer passes by keyword alone
  - title/body repetition is rejected/cleaned
  - LLM returning invalid/empty candidates no longer falls back to heuristic spam

Verification:

```text
py_compile passed
git diff --check passed
```

messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+        memory: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+        watchlist: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+        recent_feed: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+        context: Dict[str, Any],\n+        dry_decisions: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+        publish_results: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n+    ) -> str:\n+        source_context = context.get(\"source_context\") or {}\n+        posted = [result for result in publish_results if result.get(\"status\") == \"posted\"]\n+        counts = Counter(candidate.get(\"dry_decision\") or \"unknown\" for candidate in dry_decisions)\n+        lines = [\n+            \"**Live Update Dev Debug**\",\n+            f\"Run `{run_id}` via `{trigger}`. 8} more candidate(s).\")\n+        if posted:\n+            titles = \", \".join((result.get(\"candidate\") or {}).get(\"title\") or \"untitled\" for result in posted)\n+            lines.append(f\"Posted preview(s): {titles}\")\n+        else:\n+            lines.append(\"Posted preview(s): none.\")\n+        return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n+\n+    @staticmethod\n+    def _format_dry_candidate_debug(index: int, candidate: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[str]:\n+        raw = candidate.get(\"raw_agent_output\") or {}\n+        editorial = raw.get(\"editorial_decision\") or {}\n+        source_ids = \", \".join(str(item) for item in (candidate.get(\"source_message_ids\") or [])) or \"none\"\n+        rationale = candidate.get(\"rationale\") or editorial.get(\"editor_notes\") or \"\"\n+        why_now = editorial.get(\"why_now\") or \"\"\n+        duplicate = editorial.get(\"duplicate_assessment\") or \"\"\n+        context_used = editorial.get(\"context_used\") or \"\"\n+        details = [\n+            f\"{index}. `{candidate.get('dry_decision')}` / agent `{candidate.get('agent_decision', 'publish')}` / \"\n+            f\"{candidate.get('update_type')} / conf {float(candidate.get('confidence') or 0):.2f} / pri {candidate.get('priority')}\",\n+            f\"   Title: {candidate.get('title') or '(untitled)'}\",\n+            f\"   Sources: {source_ids}\",\n+        ]\n+        if rationale:\n+            details.append(f\"   Rationale: {str(rationale)[:320]}\")\n+        if why_now:\n+            details.append(f\"   Why now: {str(why_now)[:240]}\")\n+        if duplicate:\n+            details.append(f\"   Duplicate check: {str(duplicate)[:240]}\")\n+        if context_used:\n+            details.append(f\"   Context used: {str(context_used)[:240]}\")\n+        return details\n*** End Patch\n"
```json
}
```

> DEVELOPER

And are we definitely giving it all messages in this period? Can it easily search earlier messages? Can it understand it? Do we give it previous updates? Can it easily search earlier updates? Is tihs all elegantly structured?

> AGENT

Short answer: better than before, but not fully where you’re describing yet.

- **All messages in the period?** Not definitely. It fetches non-NSFW archived messages for the lookback/checkpoint window, but capped at `max_messages=200`. The prompt also includes up to `200`. If there are more than 200 messages in the window, it will not see all of them.

- **Can it easily search earlier messages?** Not as a tool. It gets pre-expanded context: same-channel history and author recent messages for selected source messages. That helps, but it cannot decide mid-turn “search further back for this topic/person.”

- **Can it understand that context?** Reasonably, yes. It receives structured JSON with source messages, editorial memory, watchlist, recent feed, and `context_expansion`. But this is still a packed prompt, not an interactive agent loop.

- **Do we give it previous updates?** Yes, recent feed items from the last `24h` are included, plus editorial memory.

- **Can it easily search earlier updates?** Not really. It gets recent feed and memory, but no arbitrary search over all old feed items. Duplicate checks also use persisted duplicate state, but the model itself cannot browse historical updates on demand.

- **Is this elegantly structured?** More than before, but not fully. Current shape is “structured prompt with preloaded context.” The elegant version is an agent/editor loop with explicit tools:
  - `get_recent_messages(window)`
  - `search_messages(query/channel/author/time_range)`
  - `get_author_profile(author_id)`
  - `get_recent_updates(hours/days)`
  - `search_previous_updates(query)`
  - `create_candidate/defer/skip/publish_decision`

Then the final agent output updates DB in prod and posts debug in dev. That’s the architecture you were originally asking for. What we have now is an interim version that should be much less stupid, but it is not yet truly “let it loose with tools.”

> DEVELOPER

is it all communicated properly to the agent?

> AGENT

Mostly, but not perfectly.

What is communicated well now:

- The old editorial bar: novelty, usefulness, evidence, attribution, no hype, skip weak items.
- What the current source window is.
- Recent feed items from the last 24h.
- Editorial memory/watchlist.
- Same-channel history and author history/stats for context-expanded messages.
- The expected output schema: decision, evidence ids, why now, duplicate assessment, context used, uncertainty, risk flags, notes.
- The posting structure: title, body, media URL, original post link.

What is not communicated well enough yet:

- That the message window may be capped at 200, so it should treat the window as “provided sample/window,” not guaranteed total community activity.
- Which messages have full expanded context versus limited context.
- That it cannot actually call tools/search further; the prompt says “use supplied context,” but does not make the limitation explicit enough.
- The relationship between normal live updates and top creations/top art sharing.
- A clear “if you need more context, defer and say exactly what to check later” instruction.
- A clean checklist it should follow before publishing.

I’d add a compact “Operating Constraints + Decision Checklist” block to the prompt so Opus knows exactly what it has and what it does not have.

> AGENT

Implemented the more agentic version and relaunched dev.

What changed:

- Source window cap is now `1000` instead of `200`.
- Opus can now take up to `6` agent turns before finalizing.
- It sees an explicit `available_tools` manifest in the prompt.
- It can request these read-only tools:
  - `search_messages`
  - `get_message_context`
  - `get_author_profile`
  - `search_previous_updates`
  - `get_recent_updates`
  - `search_editorial_memory`
- The dev debug report includes:
  - agent turn count
  - tool call count
  - each tool called, args, success/error, and result count
  - source window/channel/signal summaries
  - decisions and why it posted or stayed quiet

Nice, for this: ComfyUI PR: Multi-Threaded Model Loading for Faster Startup
A new pull request (PR #13802) on the official ComfyUI repo introduces multi-threaded loading of models from disk, targeting significant load time speedups. The PR description notes it handles both low-RAM systems (where models can't fit entirely in memory) and fast systems (where cold disk loading is the bottleneck). Community member yi shared the PR in #comfyui to enthusiastic reactions — "FINALLY", "waiting for this for so long." Not yet merged but represents a long-requested improvement to ComfyUI's model management pipeline.

Implemented the extra context/tools and relaunched dev.

What changed:
- Messages now expose reply/thread metadata clearly to Opus:
  - `reference_id`
  - `is_reply`
  - `reply_jump_url`
  - `thread_id`
  - `is_thread_message`
- `get_engagement_context` now returns richer texture:
  - parent message if the source is a reply
  - direct replies / nearby responses
  - active reactors
  - reactor/responder profiles and high-level stats
  - thread/reply flags
- Added `get_recent_reactions`:
  - shows reaction events from the last hour
  - includes the reacted-to message, its age bucket, emoji/action, and reactor profile
  - this lets older messages become candidates if they got fresh attention
- The prompt now explicitly tells Opus to use this context for reply chains, thread context, and newly validated older messages.

Verification:
- `py_compile` passed.
- Focused tests passed: `22 passed`.

> AGENT

The reason is now clear from the logs: the dev runner never got past Discord login. It hit repeated Discord `/users/@me` 429s, then a 503, and exited before the immediate pass. Since the first pass happens only after `client.login()`, it had no chance to send the launch debug/update message or schedule the hourly loop. I’m patching the runner so login failures recreate the Discord client and keep backing off through 429/5xx instead of dying.

> DEVELOPER

What do you mean hammering supabase? Surely that won't be an issue?

> AGENT

By “hammering” I mean our context builder was doing many small per-author/per-message Supabase reads in one run, not that the hourly schedule itself is too frequent. In the latest trace it fetched 285 raw messages fine, but deep enrichment for 50 selected messages triggered repeated `Server disconnected` responses from Supabase’s HTTP/2 client; reducing deep enrichment to 20 kept the run moving while still giving Opus the full raw window.

> AGENT

The more precise framing is: the query shape is inefficient. Full-window fetch is cheap; the expensive part is N selected messages times channel history, author history, author stats, engagement context, participant profiles, and more author stats. That should eventually be batched/cached, but the cap is a pragmatic guardrail while we test quality.

> AGENT

Yes, “hammering” was too loose. The issue is not the hourly run or the main message fetch. It’s the deep context enrichment doing lots of per-message/per-author Supabase requests in a burst. That caused transient `Server disconnected` warnings from the Supabase HTTP client.

I changed it so:
- the agent still gets the full raw source window: latest run saw `285` messages.
- deep enrichment is capped to the strongest `20` messages by default.
- context expansion has a timeout and won’t block the turn.
- Supabase disconnects are one-line warnings instead of huge tracebacks.
- logs now show clear phases: source fetch, context build, model turn, candidate count, publish/debug result.

Latest dev run completed successfully:
- model: `claude-opus-4-6`
- source messages: `285`
- model turn: completed in about 58s
- candidates: `1`
- accepted: `1`
- preview posted: yes
- debug report posted: yes
- top creations also completed and posted one item

The local dev runner is still running in `screen` and should run again hourly.

> DEVELOPER

And even when we're not in dev mode, do you capture/save the rationalisation?

> AGENT

I’ll verify against the persistence path rather than answer from memory, because the dev debug report and prod audit trail use different code paths.

> AGENT

Yes. In non-dev/prod mode it persists the audit trail:

- `live_update_editor_runs.metadata`: model, turn count, max turns, tool call count, tools used, source message count, context counts, tool trace summary.
- `live_update_candidates.rationale`: the candidate-level rationale.
- `live_update_candidates.raw_agent_output`: raw model output, context expansion, tool trace, raw candidate JSON, checklist, `why_now`, `new_information`, duplicate assessment, community validation, uncertainty, editor notes, etc.
- `live_update_decisions.decision_payload`: final app decision details like confidence, priority, source IDs, duplicate info, checklist failures.
- `live_update_feed_items`: what actually got posted, source IDs, media refs, Discord message IDs.

So prod should be auditable. The main caveat: if the agent returns zero candidates, we currently record run metadata, but not a rich “why I posted nothing” narrative unless it became a skipped run from no new messages. Dev has a much nicer no-post debug explanation. I’d probably add a prod-safe `no_candidate_reason` / `raw_agent_output` field on the run metadata for zero-candidate turns so quiet decisions are just as inspectable as posted ones.
